Lifecycle metadata (`closedDate`) lands with Phase 17.1 (close/reopen).

#### 8.8.2 Click pipeline (Phase 17.0)

The Directory layer renders 118 seeded RFPs (Bob's Sentinel-4 + 20 RFP-only buyer mocks + 4 mixed actors). Each RFP renders as a hollow indigo square at its world position. Phase 17.0 introduces the first interactive pipeline:

1. **Hit-test mesh.** A companion `THREE.InstancedMesh` parallels the visible outline mesh — solid `PlaneGeometry` at `RFP_BASE_OUTER`, `transparent: true; opacity: 0; depthWrite: false`. The visible outline is a ring with a centre hole, so a raycast against the outline misses interior clicks. The hit-test mesh fills that dead zone. Matrices are written in lockstep with the outline mesh's populate + rescale-on-zoom paths. Defensive InstancedMesh settings (frustumCulled = false, unbounded boundingSphere) per Round 16 lessons.
2. **Raycast** intersects both the dot mesh and the RFP hit-test mesh; closer hit wins. RFPs are placed outside cluster dots so concurrent hits are vanishingly unlikely; the closer-wins rule is defensive.
3. **Click handler** distinguishes by mesh: RFP hit → `onRfpClick(rfp)` + the standard `animatedPanToWithZoom(d.x + panelOffsetWorld, d.y, targetZoom, 500)` pan-to-center (mirror of Phase 16.1.3 Item 6 / 16.2.9 Item 1 Claim pipeline). Claim hit → existing `onClaimDotClick(claim)` flow.

#### 8.8.3 Read-only Detail Panel (Phase 17.0)

`RfpDetailPanel` (`src/components/DetailPanel/RfpDetailPanel.jsx`) mounts in V2App when `v22DirectorySelectedRfp` is non-null and the Directory is open. Panel shell positioning mirrors the Claim Detail Panel mount: `position: fixed; top: 61; right: 0; bottom: 28; width: 480; zIndex: 200` — clears the chrome at top and the footer at bottom.

Content (top → bottom):

1. **Header** — `RFP` type pillbox + status badge (`OPEN` in 17.0; green-tinted) + close button (`✕`) at the right. Followed by `rfp.name` as a large heading.
2. **Posted by** — `ACTOR` pillbox + owner party name + optional `YOU` badge when the owner is the active actor.
3. **Description** — `rfp.description` as plain prose. Muted italic "No description provided." fallback when empty.
4. **Required Standards** — chip list for each `rsId` in `rfp.requirementsSetIds`. RS metadata resolved against `publishedRequirementSets` (the same catalog Run Eval / Create Claim flows consult); chip renders "{name} v{version}" when found, falls back to the raw `rsId` under a muted treatment when unknown. Chips are visual-only in 17.0 — no click behaviour.
5. **Posted** — `rfp.createdDate` formatted as `YYYY-MM-DD · HH:MM UTC`.

No footer / action buttons. All colours via CSS variables; SVG / unicode glyphs only.

#### 8.8.4 Mutual exclusion with Claim Detail Panel (Phase 17.0)

V2App maintains two adjacent states: `v22DirectorySelectedClaim` and `v22DirectorySelectedRfp`. Mutual exclusion is enforced at the click-handler level — each setter clears the other. Both states clear on Directory close + globe-button toggle. Only one Detail Panel is visible at a time; clicking a Claim while an RFP panel is open closes the RFP panel and opens the Claim panel (and vice versa).

#### 8.8.5 Card LOD swap (Phase 17.0.1)

RFP markers transition through three visual states based on camera zoom, matching the Phase 16.2.7 Claim LOD pipeline:

| Zoom regime | Visual | Click behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| `zoom < MID_LOD_THRESHOLD` (~3.33×) | Hollow indigo square (Three.js InstancedMesh) | Raycast against invisible hit-test mesh; opens RfpDetailPanel + pins RFP tooltip near click point |
| `MID_LOD_THRESHOLD ≤ zoom < LOD_THRESHOLD` (~3.33 → 4.38×) | `AssetNodeMini` HTML overlay — type pill `RFP` + name (no owner row) | onSelect fires onCardClick → opens RfpDetailPanel + pins RFP card |
| `zoom ≥ LOD_THRESHOLD` (~4.38×) | `AssetNode` HTML overlay — type pill `RFP` + name + "Posted by {owner}" | Same as mini-card |

The visible outline mesh, the tinted fill mesh, AND the invisible hit-test mesh all hide together at `zoom ≥ MID_LOD_THRESHOLD` (so the cards don't render behind the still-present hollow squares, and so the hit-test mesh doesn't intercept clicks meant for the cards above). Claim cards and RFP cards share the same card-overlay render block in `DirectoryLayer.jsx` — each `allDots` entry resolves to either flavour based on `d.kind`.

RFP cards carry no disclosure type (RFPs are public-by-nature; no per-counterparty visibility math). The disclosure-type branches in `AssetNode`'s `borderColor` / `background` chains (Phase 16.2.10) fall through cleanly to `WARM_BORDER` for `disclosureType === undefined`. Hover/select state on RFP cards uses amber (`var(--accent-amber)`) — same as Claims, distinct from the indigo treatment non-Claim node types use on parent canvas. Rationale: WARM_BORDER is itself a 40% indigo blend, so brightening to 100% indigo on hover would not read as a clear state change. Amber against warm-grey reads cleanly.

#### 8.8.6 Pinned tooltip discriminator (Phase 17.0.1)

DirectoryLayer's `pinned` state is a **discriminated union** that carries exactly one of `claim` or `rfp` at any time. Every click path — hollow-square at low zoom, mini-card at mid-LOD, full-card at full-LOD — sets `pinned` with the appropriate shape. The pinned-tooltip render block branches on which discriminator is set and renders `ClaimTooltipCard` or `RfpTooltipCard` accordingly. Because `setPinned` overwrites the previous state in a single call, clicking from a Claim to an RFP (or vice versa) cleanly swaps the pinned tooltip — there's no race between "clear the old" and "set the new" because the single setter does both atomically. This is the structural fix for the stale-tooltip bug that 17.0 left open.

Empty-canvas click in the Directory clears both discriminators by calling `setPinned(null)` + `onClaimDotClick(null)` + `onRfpClick(null)`. V2App's handlers for `onRfpClick(null)` and `onClaimDotClick(null)` dismiss the respective Detail Panel.

#### 8.8.7 Lifecycle states (Phase 17.1)

Phase 17.1 introduces RFP lifecycle. The factory shape gains:

```
{
  ...factory fields from §8.8.1...,
  status: 'open' | 'closed',  // validated; throws on unknown
  closedDate: ISO string | null,
}
```

**State transitions** (owner-only — only the RFP's `owner` party sees the Close / Reopen buttons in the Detail Panel footer):

- `open → closed` via `closeRfp(rfp, closedDate?)`. The pure transform sets `status: 'closed'` and stamps `closedDate` (callers can override; default `new Date().toISOString()`).
- `closed → open` via `reopenRfp(rfp)`. Clears `closedDate` back to `null`.

**Session-state merge layer.** V2App maintains `v22ClosedRfpIds: Map<rfpId, ISO closedDate>`, parallel to `v22Provisionals`. The Map stores the ISO timestamp captured at click time so re-renders see a stable closedDate (regenerating it on each render would shift the displayed timestamp every paint). `mergeClosedRfps(shared, closedRfpIds)` overlays the session state on the shared artifact collection. `buildV22DirectoryDataForRole(roleId, provisionals, closedRfpIds)` chains the merge internally — no V2App call site outside the RFP code reads `shared.rfps` directly, so the merge propagates exclusively through this view-builder path.

**Asymmetric visibility model.** Closed RFPs are visible to their owner with a dashed-outline visual treatment at every Directory LOD; non-owners see them filtered out:

| Viewer | RFP status | Visible? | Visual |
|---|---|---|---|
| Owner | `open` | Yes | Solid hollow square / mini-card / full-card (Phase 17.0.1 baseline) |
| Owner | `closed` | Yes | **Dashed** outline / **dashed-border** mini-card / **dashed-border** full-card |
| Non-owner | `open` | Yes | Standard 17.0.1 rendering |
| Non-owner | `closed` | **No** | Filtered out by `buildV22DirectoryDataForRole`'s `otherRfps` / `cluster.rfps` filter |

**Implementation — dashed outline.** A single `THREE.LineSegments` mesh (`closedRfpMeshRef`) with `LineDashedMaterial`. The `BufferGeometry` is rebuilt on every layout / zoom change with 4 line segments per closed-owned RFP (square perimeter). `computeLineDistances()` is required after each rebuild for the dashing to render. The visible `rfpMesh` (solid outline) and `rfpFillMesh` (tinted fill) partition on the closed-owned condition — those instances render as `hidden` matrices there. The `rfpHitMesh` (invisible solid-square hit-test) carries ALL instances regardless of status so closed-owned RFPs remain clickable for reopen.

**Implementation — dashed card border.** At mid / full LOD, the DirectoryLayer's card-overlay render block builds a synthetic node with `isClosed: rfp.status === 'closed' && rfp.owner === activeParty`. AssetNode + AssetNodeMini's RFP early-return branches read `node.isClosed` and switch `border-style: solid → dashed` on both the inner card border and the outer selection ring. The hover-preview portal (rendered from AssetNodeMini hover) reuses the same AssetNode branch, so closed-owned mini-card hover renders a dashed full-card preview.

**Architectural rationale — why asymmetric.** A solicitation in this prototype is a notification fired by a seller at a buyer (Phase 17.2 wires this), plus the seller's already-public Claim. Once delivered, the notification persists in the buyer's inbox independent of the RFP node. Closing the RFP after a solicitation has been fired doesn't create a stale-write conflict:

- The seller no longer sees the closed RFP on their Directory (filtered out), but their existing solicitation notification persists in the buyer's inbox.
- The buyer (owner of the RFP) still sees their closed RFP with a dashed outline, can click the solicitation notification, can navigate to the RFP's Detail Panel, and can act on the solicitation (Request EA or reject).
- New solicitations can't be filed against a closed RFP because the seller no longer sees the RFP on their Directory.

This means closed-RFP visibility is intentionally asymmetric (owner sees, non-owners don't), and the seller-side flow naturally gates against closed RFPs because the entry point disappears. No additional gating logic needed when 17.2 wires solicitations.

#### 8.8.8 Forward pointers

- **Phase 17.2** — Supplier discovery + self-evaluation response: new `makeRfpResponse` factory; supplier sees open RFP on their Directory, self-evaluates against the listed RS(es), submits response. Response is a notification fired at the RFP owner.
- **Phase 17.2.1** — Buyer review surface + initiate formal Evaluation Agreement: buyer sees responses (via notifications + an RFP-level responses list), picks one, opens the existing Request EA modal pre-populated with the response's Claim + RS context.
- **Phase 17.5+** — Create-RFP entry point + post-and-edit lifecycle.

> **Prototype note — RFP visibility.** In V2.2 RFPs are seeded in `buildV22SharedArtifacts()` and rendered for every viewer of the Directory. **In production:** RFP visibility is managed via the Network's discoverability layer — published RFPs are indexed in the Public Directory; "buyer-only" or "shortlisted-supplier-only" RFPs would carry scoped visibility metadata. **Authority:** SDP (publication lifecycle) + Network discovery service.

### 8.9 RFP Solicitations (Phase 17.2+)

A **Solicitation** is a seller's introductory pointer to an RFP — "I have a Claim that may match your requirements, take a look." It is NOT a formal Evaluation Agreement; it's the cheap, reversible pre-EA stage where the seller offers a candidate and the buyer can dismiss or formalize. Solicitations live entirely inside the RfpDetailPanel; they do not render as nodes on the Directory map or on either party's parent canvas.

#### 8.9.1 Artifact shape

```
RfpSolicitation = {
  id: string,                   // 'solicit-{timestamp}-{nonce}'
  type: 'rfp-solicitation',
  rfpId: string,                // target RFP
  claimId: string,              // solicitor's existing public Claim
  solicitor: string,            // seller party (e.g., 'MicroCo')
  recipient: string,            // RFP owner party (e.g., 'GovCo')
  message: string,              // optional seller note (default '')
  status: 'pending' | 'rejected' | 'accepted',
  createdDate: ISO,             // captured at submission
  respondedDate: ISO | null,    // null until rejected/accepted
  rejectionMessage: string|null // optional buyer reply on reject
}
```

`'accepted'` validates here but isn't reachable from UI in Phase 17.2 — reserved for Phase 17.2.1's Request Agreement flow, which will route through the existing cold-path EA+DA pipeline and land a provisional Claim node on the buyer's parent canvas.

**Cardinality.** One solicitation per `(solicitor, rfpId)` pair. The picker UI enforces single-Claim-per-solicitation in 17.2; multi-Claim solicitations are deferred. Withdraw / amend an existing solicitation is also deferred — the solicitor cannot edit their own card once submitted in 17.2.

#### 8.9.2 Three-way visibility model

The RfpDetailPanel branches its solicitations section on `(viewer === owner)` and `(viewer has own solicitation)`:

| Viewer | Section above footer | Footer addition |
|---|---|---|
| RFP owner | "Incoming Solicitations (N)" + cards (or muted "No solicitations yet." when N=0) | unchanged (Close/Reopen) |
| Solicitor (non-owner, has own) | "Your Solicitation" + their single card | "Already solicited — see above." (muted) |
| Other non-owner (no own) | none | "Solicit with my Claim" affirm button |

A SolicitationCard has different action affordances per viewer:
- **Owner view + pending status** — `Request Agreement` (disabled, "Coming in Phase 17.2.1" tooltip) + `Reject` (enabled).
- **Owner view + rejected status** — no buttons (no undo in 17.2).
- **Solicitor view (any status)** — no buttons; status display only. When rejected and `rejectionMessage` is non-empty, a "Buyer's reply:" message block surfaces above the dates.

#### 8.9.3 Notification flow

Two notification types fire on state transitions; both route on click to Directory → RFP marker → RfpDetailPanel.

| Type | Recipient | Trigger | Body copy |
|---|---|---|---|
| `v22-rfp-solicitation-received` | RFP owner | Solicitor submits | "{solicitorParty} solicited your RFP — Re: {rfpName}" |
| `v22-rfp-solicitation-rejected` | Solicitor | Owner rejects | "{rejectorParty} rejected your solicitation — Re: {rfpName}.{ rejectionMessage when present}" |

Notification badge color: `received` = amber (matches the "request awaiting your action" pattern of `v22-transfer-request` / `v22-request-ea-only`); `rejected` = red (matches `v22-transfer-declined` / `v22-ea-declined`).

Click handler logic: `setV22DirectoryOpen(true)` + clear `v22DirectorySelectedClaim` (mutual exclusion) + set `v22DirectorySelectedRfp` to the target RFP (resolved from the merged shared artifacts via `mergeClosedRfps(buildV22SharedArtifacts(), v22ClosedRfpIds).rfps.find(...)`). Auto-dismissed on click — the loop terminates after rejection.

#### 8.9.4 Session-state merge layer

V2App holds `v22Solicitations: Map<solicitationId, RfpSolicitation>` (Map storage so per-id updates mutate in place via `setV22Solicitations(prev => new Map(prev).set(id, updated))`). New `mergeSolicitations(shared, solicitations)` helper appends Map values to `shared.rfpSolicitations`; returns shared unchanged on empty Map.

`buildV22SharedArtifacts` returns `rfpSolicitations: []` so consumers can rely on the field's existence. Solicitations do NOT flow through `buildV22DirectoryDataForRole` — they surface only in the RfpDetailPanel mount, which chains `mergeSolicitations` after `mergeClosedRfps + mergeProvisionals` to assemble the current artifact set.

**Persistence.** Solicitations created during a session do not persist across reload (same prototype convention as `v22Provisionals` / `v22ClosedRfpIds`). The full Reset all data flow clears `v22Solicitations` alongside `v22ClosedRfpIds`.

#### 8.9.5 Closed-RFP solicitations

If an RFP transitions from open to closed while solicitations exist on it, the owner still sees them in their panel (they need to act on them). The solicitations section renders normally. New solicitations cannot be created because the "Solicit with my Claim" button is gated on `status === 'open'`. The asymmetric closed-RFP visibility from §8.8.7 also gates against non-owners reaching a closed RFP at all — the seller-side entry point disappears.

#### 8.9.6 Accept flow (Phase 17.2.1, simplified in 17.2.1.1)

Owner-side acceptance of a pending solicitation routes the requested EA+DA pair to the RFP's bound Asset — the architectural rule that disclosure + evaluation requests must originate from an Asset (so the parent canvas can lay out the request-node + edge). Phase 17.2.1.1 made `assetId` a required field on RFPs (§8.8.1), which collapses the Phase 17.2.1 two-modal AssetPickerModal → CombinedRequestModal chain into a single direct open of CombinedRequestModal.

**Chain (Phase 17.2.1.1).**

1. **Trigger.** RFP owner (e.g. Bob) opens the RfpDetailPanel on a solicitation in `'pending'` status, clicks "Request Agreement" on the SolicitationCard. V2App captures the in-flight context in `v22AcceptingSolicitation = { solicitationId, solicitorClaimId, rfpId }`.
2. **CombinedRequestModal pre-fill (direct).** No intermediate AssetPickerModal — the handler resolves the RFP's `assetId` against `shared.assets` and opens CombinedRequestModal pre-filled via the existing mechanism (used by AI Shopper):
   - `requesterAsset` ← the Asset referenced by `rfp.assetId`, supplied as a `{ id, name, pin }` shape directly from `shared.assets`.
   - `initialPin` ← the solicitor's Claim PIN (resolves to the target Claim on Step 1 of the modal).
   - `initialRequirementsSetIds` ← the first id from the RFP's `requirementsSetIds` (further RSes are reachable via the modal's own multi-select picker, which lists all published RSes — §8.9.6.1).
   - `v22AcceptingSolicitation` stays set across this transition so the submit handler can detect the Accept entry path.
3. **Submit-side effect.** Inside `handleV22RequestSubmit`, after the existing provisional EA+DA creation, a conditional branch on `v22AcceptingSolicitation`:
   - Calls `acceptSolicitation(solicitation, pair.evaluationAgreement.id)` to transition the solicitation to `'accepted'` with `respondedDate` stamped and `acceptedEaId` linking to the new EA.
   - Writes the updated solicitation back into `v22Solicitations`.
   - Fires a `v22-rfp-solicitation-accepted` notification on the solicitor's inbox.
   - Clears `v22AcceptingSolicitation`.
   - The existing cold-path side effects (parent-canvas navigation, provisional-node pan/zoom, standard `v22-request` notification) run unchanged.
4. **Dual notification on Alice's inbox.** Both `v22-rfp-solicitation-accepted` (informational, click routes to RFP panel) AND the standard `v22-request` (actionable, click routes to the new EA+DA on Alice's parent canvas) are delivered. The two coexist intentionally: the first surfaces the solicitation status transition for posterity; the second is the live actionable artifact for the rest of the EA+DA lifecycle.

**`acceptedEaId` linkage.** The `makeRfpSolicitation` factory accepts an optional `acceptedEaId` field (default null). Set by `acceptSolicitation`. Future work (e.g. solicitor-side "open the resulting EA" deep-link from the accepted SolicitationCard) reads this id to resolve the EA artifact.

**Cold-path preservation.** The existing CombinedRequestModal entry point (Claim PIN click on the parent canvas, AI Shopper result) leaves `v22AcceptingSolicitation` null, so the submit-side conditional branch is a no-op — cold-path behaviour is bit-identical to before 17.2.1.

**AssetPickerModal component.** Phase 17.2.1's AssetPickerModal stays in the codebase (`src/components/modals/AssetPickerModal.jsx`) for future use by the create-RFP flow (Phase 17.5+) where the buyer picks which Asset their new RFP binds to. The V2App mount + handlers were removed from the Accept flow path in 17.2.1.1; the component itself was not deleted.

#### 8.9.6.1 Claim greying in SolicitationCreateModal (Phase 17.2.1.1)

When a seller (e.g. Alice) opens the SolicitationCreateModal against a buyer's (e.g. Bob's) RFP, the Claim picker greys out any Claim that's already mapped to the buyer via an active or pending Evaluation Agreement. The predicate is:

```
isClaimOnBuyerNetwork(claimId) =
  ∃ ea ∈ shared.evaluationAgreements:
    ea.grantee.party === rfp.owner &&
    ea.grantor.party === activeRole.party &&
    ea.subject.kind === 'claim' &&
    ea.subject.id === claimId &&
    !ea._declineMeta && !ea._revokedMeta
```

A greyed row renders at `opacity: 0.45` with `cursor: not-allowed`, no click handler, an "ON NETWORK" pillbox, and a tooltip "Already on {ownerParty}'s network". Submit gating blocks selection of greyed Claims defensively.

**Rationale.** Without this gate, the seller could submit a solicitation pointing at a Claim that's already disclosed to the buyer. The buyer's Accept flow would then error because the cold-path CombinedRequestModal flags the Claim's PIN as `already-disclosed` (Phase 11D #134). Surfacing the impossible state at the solicitation entry point lets the seller pick a different Claim before the cycle starts.

#### 8.9.6.2 EA+DA notification Directory close (Phase 17.2.1.1)

When the active actor receives an EA+DA notification (`v22-request`, `v22-request-ea-only`, `v22-ea-accepted`, `v22-ea-declined`) while currently viewing the Directory layer, clicking the notification closes the Directory overlay before running cold-path navigation. Previously the Directory stayed open behind the response modal / over the parent canvas. This is a small UX fix that ensures the user always lands on their parent canvas at the target artifact when responding to an EA+DA event.

#### 8.9.6.3 CombinedRequestModal RS scrollbox (Phase 17.2.1.1)

CombinedRequestModal's Step 1 Requirements Set scrollbox lists **all published Requirements Sets** (network-wide), not just the active actor's own RS pool. Each published row renders:

- Globe icon (canonical `GlobeIcon`, matches LibraryModal / BadgesPanel / RequirementsPanel)
- RS name + version
- "Published by {owner party}" muted line below

Own (non-published) RSes are appended below the published rows with their pre-17.2.1.1 minimal treatment (no globe, no owner line — just name + id + version). Dedupe by id (own wins so own-private RSes can never disappear).

**Pre-selection.** The existing `initialRequirementsSetIds` prop wire-through works against the expanded data source — RFP-bound pre-selection (Accept flow) now finds the RFP's referenced RSes because they're present in the list. The cold-path entry (Claim PIN click on parent canvas) is unaffected — same modal, same per-row rendering, no RFP pre-selection.

#### 8.9.6.4 Solicitation-context UI — Bob's side (Phase 18.3.1)

The RFP owner's (Bob's) side of the solicitation flow. The seller (Alice) creating a solicitation is §8.9 + the Phase 18.3 DA mint; this subsection is the buyer's discovery + response surface.

**The `_solicitationContext` adapter stamp.** A pending solicitation offers a Claim to the buyer via a directed Disclosure Agreement carrying the `_solicitationId` / `_rfpId` stamps (Phase 18.3 `handleCreateSolicitation`). The module-scoped predicate `computeSolicitationContext(claim, activeParty, disclosureAgreements, solicitationsMap)` returns `{ solicitationId, rfpId }` when such a DA targets the viewer **and** the linked solicitation is still `'pending'`; null otherwise. **Pending-only gating is intentional** — after acceptance the solicitation's role is complete (the EA takes over via §8.9.6) and the Reject affordance no longer applies; after rejection the DA is revoked (cascade) and the predicate's `!d._revokedMeta` clause fails. The result is stamped onto the Claim **node** as `_solicitationContext` at *both* mount paths, because a solicited Claim can surface on either surface:

- **`v22DataWithReveal`** (parent canvas) — added to the memo's `anyDecoration` gate, the per-node early-return guard, the returned spread, and the deps array (`v22Solicitations`). Fires only for non-owned Claims that actually appear on the active actor's parent canvas.
- **Directory mount `syntheticNode`** — the directory-materialized Claim panel (the primary surface, since solicitation DAs are directed/umbrella DAs that surface on the Directory, not the parent canvas per §17.5.0.4). This is the node the notification-routing path (below) lands on.
- **Directory card synthetic node** (Phase 18.3.1.1) — the dot/card in the solicitor's cluster (built in `DirectoryLayer.jsx` alongside the `_directory*` EA-state stamps). 18.3.1 missed this third surface (Deviation #2), so the V22ActionBar Reject icon never appeared on the solicited Claim's card; 18.3.1.1 adds the parallel stamp (inlined predicate, array form of `solicitations`) + a `rejectSolicitation` branch in V2App's `onClaimCardAction` dispatcher (the Directory card routes through `onClaimCardAction`, not the parent-canvas `onV22CardAction` switch). Issue Badge is now hidden on every Directory card (its dispatch isn't wired for Directory synthetic nodes — was a dead affordance).

**Reject affordance (additive).** When `_solicitationContext` is set, both the V22ClaimPanel footer (a `✕` "Reject Solicitation" `FooterButton`) and the V22ActionBar CLAIM card (a `✕` icon) surface a Reject entry — universal footer↔action-bar parity. Critically, Reject is **additive**, not a replacement: the existing Phase 11C warm-path "Request Evaluation Agreement" (`▷`) continues to render because a solicitation DA is structurally a directed DA without a paired EA, satisfying the `_hasActiveDaWithoutEa` / `hasWarmPathAction` predicate by identity. So Bob sees **both** `▷` Request EA and `✕` Reject on a solicited Claim. Reject opens the existing `SolicitationRejectModal` → `handleRejectSolicitation`, which since Phase 18.3 cascade-revokes the linked DA (the offered Claim then disappears from Bob's view) and notifies the solicitor. No new modal or handler — Phase 18.3.1 only adds two entry points to the existing downstream flow.

**Notification routing change.** `v22-rfp-solicitation-received` clicks now route the receiver to the **offered Claim** (Directory → Claim panel, via `handleSelectSolicitation` → `routeToPublishedClaim(claimId)`) rather than the RFP marker. The receiver's primary task on click is Claim-centric — review the scope, request an EA, or reject — and the Claim panel surfaces those affordances directly. The four other RFP notification types (`v22-rfp-solicitation-rejected` / `-accepted` / `-closed` / `-reopened`) are split into a separate branch that continues routing to the RFP marker unchanged (the solicitor's rejection/acceptance and the owner's close/reopen are RFP-centric). `claimId` was added to the received notification's payload in Phase 18.3 precisely for this routing.

#### 8.9.7 Forward pointers

- **Phase 17.6+** — Solicitation withdraw / amend (seller-side); multi-Claim solicitations per (seller, RFP) pair; solicitor-side "open the resulting EA" deep-link from accepted SolicitationCard via `acceptedEaId`.

### 8.10 Directory-layer Claim CTAs (Phase 17.3)

The Directory layer's Claim discovery surface (panel + full-size card) presents the active actor with state-dependent next-action affordances:

| State | Panel body callout | Panel footer button | Card action bar button |
|---|---|---|---|
| Active actor is the Claim owner | (none) | (none for EA) | (none for EA) |
| Non-owner, no DA, no EA | "An Evaluation Agreement is required to evaluate this Claim." (amber) | Request Evaluation Agreement | Request Evaluation Agreement (▷) |
| Non-owner, no DA, EA exists | "An Evaluation Agreement is in place with {owner}." (indigo) | View Evaluation Agreement | View Evaluation Agreement (◉) |
| Non-owner, DA exists, no EA (warm-path, Phase 11C) | "An Evaluation Agreement is required to evaluate this Claim." (amber) | Request Evaluation Agreement (warm-path) | Request Evaluation Agreement (▷) (`_hasActiveDaWithoutEa`) |

The two new states (no DA + no EA, no DA + EA exists) are Phase 17.3 additions; the warm-path row is the Phase 11C precedent.

**Shared predicate.** `getActiveEaForClaimAndRequester(claim, requesterParty, evaluationAgreements)` exported from `v2_2Data.js` resolves the EA between the active actor (grantee) and the Claim's owner (grantor). Filters: matches `grantor.party === claim.owner`, `grantee.party === requesterParty`, `claimId === claim.id`; excludes EAs flagged `_declineMeta` or `_revokedMeta`. Tie-break (multiple matches): prefer `active` over `pending-acceptance`, then most recent by `terms.createdDate`.

**Cold-path Request EA flow (no DA, no EA).** Symmetric to the Phase 17.2.1 RFP Accept flow but without an RFP — the user picks one of their own Assets to anchor the request via AssetPickerModal, then the existing CombinedRequestModal opens pre-filled with the picked Asset + the target Claim's PIN. AssetPickerModal's prop contract is generalized (Phase 17.3): accepts a `targetClaim` + `context` (`{ type: 'directory-claim' }` | `{ type: 'rfp', rfp }`) — context drives the modal's subtitle and context block copy. Legacy solicitation/solicitorClaim/rfp props are preserved for any future re-use should the AssetPickerModal step return to the create-RFP flow.

**View EA flow (EA exists).** Closes the Directory overlay, clears any open Claim / RFP panel, then on the parent canvas: pans + zooms to the Claim node, selects the EA edge (matched via `pairedEvaluationAgreementId`), opens the EA Detail Panel via the existing `setOpenAgreement({ kind: 'evaluation', evaluationAgreementId })` mechanism. Mirrors the Phase 11C / Phase 17.2.1.1 EA-amendment notification routing pattern. Assumes the EA is already on the active actor's parent canvas — the predicate's filter guarantees this for active EAs.

**Card vs. panel parity.** Both surfaces (V22NodeDetailPanel's footer and AssetNode's V22ActionBar on Directory full-size Claim cards) fire the same V2App handlers (`handleRequestEaForClaim`, `handleViewEa`). DirectoryLayer's per-card render path stamps two markers on the synthetic Claim node — `_directoryRequestEaCandidate` (no-EA cold path) and `_directoryExistingEa` (EA artifact when present) — which V22ActionBar consults to choose the CTA. Parent-canvas Claim cards never receive these stamps, so the parent CTAs remain the existing Run Evaluation / Amend Claim / Self-Evaluate / Issue Badge set.

**Parent-layer EA-required message bug.** A pre-existing bug where the parent-canvas Claim panel surfaces "An Evaluation Agreement is required to evaluate this Claim." in cases where it shouldn't (e.g. when an EA is on the way via a provisional or pending acknowledgment chain) is **deferred to Phase 17.4+** alongside the umbrella DA edge work — the architectural correction lives in that scope.

**#202 — ACTOR / ASSET label cleanup (Phase 17.3).** The `PanelHeader`'s `typeLabel` prop is now optional. V22ActorPanel + V22AssetPanel omit it; their content (Actor name + PIN, or Asset name + PIN + file chip) is self-identifying. CLAIM / EVAL RESULT / PARSE RESULT / PROOF OF EVALUATION / BADGE TEMPLATE panels keep their type pill since those are abstract artifact concepts that benefit from an explicit label. Relational labels (e.g. "Posted by" + ACTOR pillbox on RfpDetailPanel, "For Asset" + ASSET pillbox) are explicitly preserved — they convey a relationship, not a category, and the inline pillbox is the self-identifying chip.

### 8.11 RFP Creation flow (Phase 17.5.1)

The cold-path complement to §8.9.6's Accept flow: an active actor *posts* a new RFP rather than responding to a solicitation against an existing one. This is the third leg of the Directory-layer triad (browse §8.2 + connect §8.10 + create §8.11).

**Entry.** From one of the active actor's own Assets — the "Create RFP" action (`⬚` icon) on the Asset card's V22ActionBar or the Asset Detail Panel footer (entry points shipped Phase 17.5). Both dispatch `handleCreateRfp(assetNode)`, which opens `RfpCreationModal` pre-anchored to the clicked Asset (`v22RfpCreationContext = { asset }`). The anchor Asset is fixed for the modal's lifetime and pinned in the header copy ("Create RFP for {asset.name}").

**Modal — two steps** (`RfpCreationModal.jsx`, mirrors CombinedRequestModal's StepDots + Cancel/Continue/Back/Submit footer):
- **Step 1 — Form.** RFP name (single-line, required), description (textarea, required), Requirements Sets (multi-select scrollbox, ≥1 required). The scrollbox renders two sections — **Published Requirements Sets** (globe icon + "Published by {owner}") then **Your Requirements Sets** (minimal) — from the shared `v22AvailableRequirementsSetRows` (dedupe by id, published first). Continue is gated on `name.trim() && description.trim() && selectedRsIds.size > 0`.
- **Step 2 — Review/Confirm.** Labeled-list card (For Asset / RFP Name / Description / Requirements Sets chips). Back returns to Step 1 preserving all form state; Submit fires `onSubmit({ name, description, requirementsSetIds })` and closes the modal.

**Submit → artifact.** `handleRfpCreationSubmit` builds the RFP via `makeRfp` — `id = makeArtifactId('rfp', '{party}-{name-slug}-{Date.now()}')` (slug + timestamp guarantees uniqueness across same-session creations), `owner`/`ownerDot` from `activeRole`, `assetId` from the anchor Asset, `status: 'open'`, `createdDate: now`. The new RFP is added to V2App's `v22CreatedRfps` Map<id, rfp> session state (mirror of `v22ClosedRfpIds`).

**Session-state merge layer.** `mergeCreatedRfps(shared, createdRfps)` (new `v2_2Data.js` export, pure, mirrors `mergeClosedRfps`) appends the user-created RFPs to `shared.rfps`. `buildV22DirectoryDataForRole`'s signature is extended to `(roleId, provisionals, closedRfpIds, createdRfps)` and its merge chain runs **created → closed** (`mergeClosedRfps(mergeCreatedRfps(mergeProvisionals(...), createdRfps), closedRfpIds)`) so a freshly-created RFP can be Closed within the same session (mergeClosedRfps' `.map` runs over the merged `rfps` and finds the created one if its id is in `closedRfpIds`). The created RFP then flows through the per-role filter exactly like a seeded RFP — it appears in the creating actor's own cluster (`ownRfps`) and, while `open`, is network-discoverable for solicitation (visible in the owner's cluster on other actors' Directory views, like any open seed RFP).

**Scope boundary.** Post-creation orchestration — layer switch (Parent → Directory) + pan/zoom to the new RFP node + auto-open Detail Panel + "NEW" badge — is deferred to Phase 17.5.2. In 17.5.1 the modal simply closes on Submit; the user navigates to the Directory manually. The parent-canvas view-builder (`buildViewForActor`) is unchanged — RFPs are Directory-layer-only artifacts.

**Implicit publication via reference (Phase 17.5.1.1).** Any Requirements Set referenced by any RFP must resolve in the RFP Detail Panel — and in the SolicitationCreateModal accordion — regardless of its publication status. The RFP's Requirements list IS the public interface for those RSes: by naming a private RS in a network-visible RFP, the owner has implicitly published it to anyone who can see the RFP. V2App computes a dedicated `v22RfpRsLookupPool` (all published RSes + every RFP-referenced RS resolved against the network-wide seed pool `DEMO_REQUIREMENT_SETS` + the active actor's own RSes) and passes it to both panels for chip resolution. This pool is *resolution-only* — it does NOT widen the user-facing RS picker for new RFPs (which still offers published + own via `v22AvailableRequirementsSetRows`), nor the Library's published list. The RS picker (Phase 17.5.1.1) also orders Your RSes first; an own RS that is also published stays in the Your section with a globe icon + a publication tooltip rather than duplicating into the Published section.

**Directory cluster integration (Phase 17.5.1.2).** Every RFP owner is rendered as exactly one Directory cluster on every viewer's Directory, holding all of that owner's RFPs (seeded + user-created) under a single cluster label. `buildV22DirectoryDataForRole` classifies RFPs as follows: (a) attach each owner's RFPs to their existing (Claim-bearing) cluster if one is present on the viewer's Directory; (b) upsert an RFP-only cluster for every remaining RFP owner — including the primary switchable parties (GovCo / MicroCo / AuditCo / ChipCo); (c) route to the orphan pool (`otherRfps`) ONLY owners that still lack a cluster (normally none). The prior `PRIMARY_PARTIES_FOR_ORPHAN_RFP` carve-out forced primary-party RFPs through the orphan path, where DirectoryLayer renders one owner-pillbox-label *per orphan marker* (`clusterIdx === -1`); a buyer like GovCo (no public Claims → no Claim cluster) with two RFPs (seeded Sentinel-4 + a user-created one) therefore produced two "GovCo" labels. The Phase 17.5.1.1 orphan-spread positioning is preserved as the fallback for genuine clusterless orphans. **RFP Detail Panel RS rows** render in a two-line layout (Phase 17.5.1.2): line 1 = RS name + canonical `GlobeIcon` (shown whenever the RS is in `publishedRequirementSets`, regardless of owner or viewer) + version pill; line 2 = the RS owner party name in muted secondary text (no "YOU" pill — the panel header already carries the YOU indicator for the RFP itself). The `v22RfpRsLookupPool` entries carry derived `owner` + `isPublished` fields for this; the RS chip click handler resolves against the same pool (single source for rendering and click), so own-private RFP-referenced RSes are clickable. CombinedRequestModal and SolicitationCreateModal retain their existing one-line RS sectioning.

**RFP lifecycle — Close notification + Remove (Phase 17.5.1.4).** The owner-side RFP lifecycle is `open → closed → removed` (Close and Reopen are reversible; Remove is terminal). **Close** (panel footer or card action bar, owner-only) records the closure in `v22ClosedRfpIds` AND fires a `v22-rfp-closed` notification to every *unique* solicitor party of the RFP (deduped; zero solicitors → zero notifications). The notification badge is muted-indigo; click routes Directory → closed RFP marker → RfpDetailPanel (solicitor view) mirroring §8.9.6's solicitation-received routing. **Reopen** ~~is silent (no re-notification — the close notification is the asymmetric lifecycle signal)~~ — **superseded by Phase 17.5.2.2:** Reopen now fires a `v22-rfp-reopened` notification to every unique solicitor party (symmetric with Close), so solicitors learn an RFP they've solicited is actionable again. **Remove** is available ONLY on `'closed'` RFPs (Andrew's explicit constraint: Close must happen first so solicitors are notified; Remove is the silent cleanup afterward). It adds the id to `v22RemovedRfpIds`; `mergeRemovedRfps` (applied LAST in the Directory merge chain) filters the RFP out of `shared.rfps` and cascades to drop every `rfpSolicitations` entry referencing it. No notification fires on Remove. The removed RFP disappears from every actor's Directory on next render, and any stale solicitation-received / close notifications simply fail to resolve a target RFP on click (acceptable — the loop has terminated). The Remove UI is surfaced as a danger-variant `ActionButton` in the Detail Panel footer (alongside Reopen) and a trash-icon entry on the full-LOD card action bar (`_directoryRemoveCandidate`, gated on owner + closed). Close/Reopen state refreshes in-place because the panel mount re-derives `currentRfp` from a merge chain that now includes `mergeCreatedRfps` + `mergeRemovedRfps` (Phase 17.5.1.4 Fix A — previously a user-created RFP wasn't in the merged set, so its status never flipped without deselect/reselect).

**RFP management surfaces (Phase 17.5.1.5).** There are now three surfaces for managing an RFP's lifecycle, all wired to the same `handleCloseRfp` / `handleReopenRfp` / `handleRemoveRfp` handlers: (1) the **RfpDetailPanel footer** (Directory layer), (2) the **full-LOD card action bar** (Directory layer), and (3) the **Asset Detail Panel "Anchored RFPs" section** (parent canvas). Surface (3) is the only parent-canvas place to see RFPs at all (RFPs are Directory-layer artifacts), so it doubles as the discovery surface: V22AssetPanel lists every RFP whose `assetId` matches the Asset (newest-first, from the full merge chain), each row click-navigating to the Directory via `routeToRfp(rfpId)`. Owner rows carry inline Close (open) or Reopen + Remove (closed) `ActionLabel`s; non-owner rows are identity + navigation only. Because all three surfaces share the handlers, closing from a row fires solicitor notifications and removing from a row silently cascades — identical to the Directory surfaces.

**Post-creation orchestration + bidirectional navigation (Phase 17.5.2 — closes the RFP creation arc).** Three transitions complete the navigation model. (a) **Post-creation:** submitting a new RFP fires `routeToRfp(newRfp.id, { rfp: newRfp })` — switches Parent → Directory (forward wipe), pans/zooms to the new marker at full-card LOD, auto-opens its Detail Panel — and stamps `v22RecentlyCreatedRfpId` so the synthetic card renders a NEW badge (`_isNew`, threaded via the `recentlyCreatedRfpId` prop) until the user navigates away (clear-on-deselect effect). (b) **Directory → Parent (RFP → Asset):** the RfpDetailPanel "For Asset" row is clickable, routed through `routeToAsset(assetId)` — the inverse of (3) above — closing the Directory + Detail Panels and selecting/pan-zooming to the Asset on the Parent canvas. (c) **Directory → Parent (escape):** double-clicking empty canvas space on the Directory triggers the reverse circle wipe (same as the globe button / exit chip), filtered to settled-phase + canvas-target + raycast-miss so markers, cluster labels, cards, and chrome don't trigger it. Together with the click-to-open-RFP routing (17.5.1.5), the Parent↔Directory navigation is now bidirectional from both the Asset side and the RFP side.

### 8.12 Publish Claim to Directory flow (Phase 18.2; modal consolidated in 18.2.2; orchestration + already-published gate in 18.2.3)

The seller-side counterpart to the RFP buyer-posting flow (§8.8): the Claim owner publishes one of their own Claims to the Public Directory. This restores the V2.0/V2.1 "publish to the Radiant Network" mechanic lost in the V2.2 Directory rebuild.

**Entry points (owner only).** Two surfaces, per the universal V2.2 rule that every Detail Panel footer action also exists on the node's V22ActionBar: (1) the **Claim Detail Panel footer** ("Publish to Directory", icon `⊕`, after Amend Claim + Self-Evaluate); (2) the **Claim card action bar** CLAIM branch (`onV22CardAction('publishToDirectory')`). Both are gated on `node.owner === activeRole.party`. The Directory-layer Claim panel intentionally omits the handler (Phase 17.4.4: the Directory Claim panel is a grantee-side/view-only surface).

**Three-step modal — `CombinedResponseModal` with `directoryPublishMode={true}` (Phase 18.2.2).** The publish flow is structurally "disclose to the Radiant Network as a non-human auto-accepting grantee" — identical to the cold-path Disclosure Response (§7.1) minus the EA half — so it reuses the same modal rather than a bespoke one. The bespoke `PublishToDirectoryModal` shipped in 18.2 is retired. A `directoryPublishMode` prop branches the modal's existing 4-step flow into **3 steps: Type → Scope → Review** — no Decline card (the Step 1 grid drops 4 cols → 3), no EA Terms step, no Acknowledgments row. Step copy is publish-specific (header "Publish to the Public Directory" / "Review your Public Directory Disclosure"; banner shows "Publishing to / Radiant Network" instead of "Requester / {party}"; scope-step descriptions reference "the Public Directory" / "Actors browsing the Public Directory" instead of a counterparty name). On submit the modal fires `onAccept({ type, scope, daTerms: { expires } })` — a DA-only payload, no `eaTerms`. V2App mounts it with a synthesized `request` shape (active actor as `ownerParty`, `'Radiant Network'` as `requesterParty`, `proposedEaTerms: null`) and the same `referencedAssets` / `parseResults` / `poesForClaim` prop contract as the cold-path response mount. Parity-by-construction: future improvements to the response modal land on the publish surface too.

**Discriminated scope shape per disclosure type** (all carry `subject: { kind: 'claim', id }`, `grantee = Radiant Network`):
- **Full** → `scope.assetIds = [picked Assets]`; `fieldIds`/`poeIds = null`, `includeDerivatives: true`. Picker = checkbox list of the Claim's owner-held referenced Assets. Defaults to zero-selected (the CombinedResponseModal picker convention per CLAUDE.md — the user explicitly opts in; this differs from the retired modal's all-checked default).
- **Selective** → `scope.fieldIds = [picked composite ids]` (composite id `${parseResultId}::${fieldId}`, built inside the modal per the §11D.2 convention); `poeIds = null`, `includeDerivatives: false`. Picker = parsed-field checkboxes grouped per Parse Result.
- **Proof-Only** → `scope.poeIds = [picked PoEs]`; `assetIds`/`fieldIds = null`. Per Phase 11D.3, proof-only public Claim DAs use `subject.kind === 'claim'` + `scope.poeIds` (distinct from the proof-of-evaluation DA shape, which uses `subject.kind === 'poe'`). The PoE picker lists every PoE on the Claim regardless of evaluator (Claim-membership filter only, per Phase 18.2.1.1 — third-party-authored PoEs are valid scope candidates), each row showing `by {owner}` attribution. The empty-state card ("No Proofs of Evaluation available…") renders when the Claim has no PoEs (inherited from the cold-path Step 2 Proof-Only branch).

**Radiant Network as auto-accepting grantee — no provisional/accept lifecycle.** Unlike a party-to-party Disclosure Agreement (which is minted `provisional` and requires the counterparty to accept), publishing to the Directory mints the DA directly with its real `type` (`full`/`selective`/`proofonly`) and `status: 'active'`. The Radiant Network is a directory, not an actor that accepts/declines — so there's no two-sided handshake. The handler (`handlePublishToDirectorySubmit`) reads `payload.daTerms?.expires`, mints the DA via `makeDisclosureAgreement`, and pushes it into `v22Provisionals.disclosureAgreements`; `mergeProvisionals` merges it by id and `buildV22DirectoryDataForRole`'s existing public-DA detection (`grantee.party === 'Radiant Network'` + `subject.kind === 'claim'` + `type !== 'provisional'`) surfaces the Claim as a dot in the active actor's own cluster on next Directory render. **No new merge helper, session-state Map, or builder signature change** — publishing is just a well-formed DA push into the existing provisionals container.

**Already-published gate (Phase 18.2.3).** Both entry surfaces disable the Publish action once the Claim already carries an active Radiant-Network-grantee DA — an already-published Claim must have its existing public DA revoked before it can be re-published. `v22DataWithReveal` stamps `_publishedToDirectory: true` on owned Claim nodes whose Claim has such a DA (predicate: `subject.kind === 'claim'` + `grantor.party === activeParty` + `grantee.party === 'Radiant Network'` + `type !== 'provisional'` + not declined/revoked — same shape as the §18.2.1.1 PoE predicate). The V22ClaimPanel footer button and the V22ActionBar CLAIM-branch button both read the flag and render disabled with the tooltip "This Claim is already published to the Public Directory. Revoke the existing Disclosure Agreement in the Agreements section to republish." (the action bar uses the shorter "This Claim is already published to the Public Directory."). The flag clears reactively when the DA is revoked because `v22View` recomputes from `v22Provisionals`. A defensive no-op gate in V2App's `publishToDirectory` dispatch case guards any path that renders the action active on an already-published Claim.

**Post-publish orchestration (Phase 18.2.3).** On submit, `handlePublishToDirectorySubmit` stamps `v22RecentlyPublishedClaimId` (drives the NEW badge) and fires `routeToPublishedClaimRef.current(claim.id)`. `routeToPublishedClaim` mirrors `routeToRfp` byte-for-byte: it closes any parent-canvas selection, opens the Directory, sets the selected Directory Claim (auto-opening its Detail Panel), and runs a 240-frame rAF retry loop calling DirectoryLayer's imperative `selectClaim(claim, { zoom: 0.6 })` until the just-published dot appears in the rebuilt layout. `selectClaim` (parallel to `selectRfp`) resolves the Claim dot via `layoutRef.allDots.filter(kind !== 'rfp')` and calls a new `focusClaimInternal` helper (pins the dot + pans/zooms with panel-offset compensation). The 60% landing zoom keeps cluster context visible (the dot reads as a dot at that LOD; the NEW badge — stamped `_isNew` on the matching synthetic Claim card — renders at card-LOD when the user zooms in, same as the RFP NEW badge). A clear-on-deselect effect drops `v22RecentlyPublishedClaimId` once the selected Directory Claim is no longer the published one. Mirrors the RFP creation arc's 17.5.1 → 17.5.2 split.

### 8.13 Solicitations carry a Disclosure Agreement (Phase 18.3)

Phase 18.3 restructures RFP solicitations so each one carries a paired Disclosure Agreement from the solicitor to the RFP owner, scoped to the offered Claim. The DA gives the owner real visibility into the Claim's contents instead of just a message + reference, so the full demo arc (post solicitation → owner sees the Claim, reviews disclosure → requests EA → solicitor accepts) works without the owner blind-requesting an Agreement. **This phase ships the architecture + creation flow; the owner-side UI (RFP Detail Panel solicitation rows, Claim Detail Panel solicitation-context actions, warm-path Request EA wiring, notification routing, Reject cascade UI) lands in Phase 18.3.1.**

**The solicitation-DA shape + link-back.** A DA is minted atomically with each solicitation: `grantor` = solicitor, `grantee` = RFP owner, `subject: { kind: 'claim', id }`, `type`/`scope` per the modal selections, `status: 'active'`, plus link-back stamps `_solicitationId` + `_rfpId`. The solicitation gains a `disclosureAgreementId` field (added to `makeRfpSolicitation`) pointing at the DA, so lookups are O(1) in both directions. The DA lands in `v22Provisionals.disclosureAgreements` (same container as Phase 18.2's publish DAs); the solicitation lands in the `v22Solicitations` Map. Both are pushed in one `handleCreateSolicitation` call.

**Owner-side visibility = the existing directed/umbrella claim-DA path.** A solicitation DA is structurally a *directed claim DA to the owner* (grantee = owner, grantor = a non-owner non-network party, subject.kind = claim) — identical in shape to the Phase 16.0/17.4 umbrella DAs. So `buildV22DirectoryDataForRole`'s existing umbrella pass (`umbrellaClaimIdsByGrantor`) already pulls the offered Claim into the solicitor's cluster on the owner's Directory (upserting the cluster if the solicitor has none), and the umbrella outline wraps it — **no separate directed-DA cluster pass is needed.** Likewise the directed-vs-public *override* is automatic: a viewer's `partyDisclosureAgreements` (in `buildViewForActor`) only ever contains DAs where the viewer is grantor/grantee, so a Radiant-Network public DA on the same Claim is never in the owner's set to conflict — the directed DA's scope is the only one the owner sees. (Caveat: in the current seed every Claim is publicly disclosed to the Radiant Network, so the cluster *appear/disappear* effect isn't observable for seeded Claims — every Claim is already a dot. The architecturally meaningful, verified effects are the atomic DA mint, the directed scope, and the cascade revoke below.)

**Cascade revoke.** Three triggers revoke the linked DA, dropping the offered Claim's directed agreement from the owner's view: (1) **solicitation rejected** — `handleRejectSolicitation` transitions the solicitation to `rejected` and calls the shared `revokeSolicitationDa(linkedDaId, 'solicitation_rejected')`, which stamps `status: 'revoked'` + `_revokedMeta`; (2) **RFP closed** — `handleCloseRfp` now rejects every open (pending) solicitation on the RFP and revokes each linked DA via the same `revokeSolicitationDa` (no per-solicitation rejected notification — the `v22-rfp-closed` notification already informs each solicitor); (3) **DA expiry** — the DA's `terms.expires` is the source of truth; existing DA-expiry logic handles it (no new code). A revoked DA is excluded from the directory builder's `activeDas` filter and from the owner's view, so the directed agreement disappears (the dot persists only if a public DA still covers the Claim).

---

## 9. AI Shopper

The AI Shopper is a tool exposed via an icon in the canvas chrome, similar to the Library and Notifications. It is not a node on the network.

- **Input:** a Requirements Set (from the user's library) + a context prompt (free text).
- **Output:** a notification containing a results panel of candidate Claims discovered in the Public Directory.
- **UI surface:**
  - Icon in the chrome (always present).
  - Modal for launching a search.
  - Background-agent-at-work animation (for demo purposes, a fake 10–30 second progress sequence is acceptable).
  - Results panel accessible from notifications.
- **From results, user can:**
  - View a Claim's public-directory Detail Panel (owner, description, posted date, aggregate stats).
  - Request a bundled Disclosure + Evaluation Agreement from that Claim.

Like the Directory Layer, AI Shopper is a back-burner item. Its existence is specified for reference, but its implementation follows the core migration.

> **Prototype note — AI Shopper.** The prototype runs a scripted progress animation (10–30s fake agent-at-work sequence) and returns mocked candidate results from a pre-seeded list of public-directory Claims. **In production:** a real LLM-backed agent runs queries against the Platform's public directory index, producing real discovery results. Search infrastructure, per-Claim discoverability metadata, and agent orchestration are all Platform concerns. **Authority:** Platform (directory index + search); External Service (LLM agent execution). Backlog #47.

---

## 10. JSON Schemas

> **Prototype note — Schema fidelity.** All schemas are mocked for the prototype. The JSON below captures the shape of each artifact as the App constructs and consumes it client-side. **In production:** each artifact is cryptographically signed by the issuing actor's DID; Platform-issued fields (PINs, DOTs, timestamps, status values) are populated by DPP/SDP/REP/COP at registration rather than by the App; Merkle proofs, on-chain anchoring, and agreement-lifecycle state fields ride along with each artifact. Per-field authority — which fields are Platform-issued vs App-derived — is called out in the subsections below where relevant and consolidated in §18. **Authority:** DPP (issuance of identity and registration fields); respective protocols (SDP/REP/COP) for lifecycle/state fields.

### 10.1 Asset artifact

> **Prototype note — Asset field authority.** Platform-issued fields in production: `pin`, `ownerDot`, `file.hash` (verified at ingest), `registrationDate`. App-derived / actor-input fields: `name`, `description`, `file.uri`, `file.filename`, `file.size`, `file.mimeType`, `parentAssetId`. **Prototype-only field:** `file.localPath` — pointer to a placeholder PDF in `/public/` so the Detail Panel's expand-evidence iframe has something real to render. **In production:** files are resolved via Platform-side QS URI lookups against `file.uri`; there is no `localPath`. **Authority:** DPP (Platform-issued fields); Actor + App (actor-input fields); App-only (`localPath`).

```json
{
  "artifactType": "asset",
  "artifactUri": "provenance://assets/{id}",
  "pin": "PIN-0x...",
  "owner": "MicroCo",
  "ownerDot": "DOT-0x...",
  "name": "Power Regulation Module Datasheet",
  "description": "Official datasheet for PRM-3A rev. 4",
  "parentAssetId": null,
  "file": {
    "uri": "provenance://evidence/prm-datasheet-v4",
    "filename": "prm-datasheet-v4.pdf",
    "size": 2458792,
    "mimeType": "application/pdf",
    "hash": "sha256:...",
    "localPath": "/powerregulationmodule-datasheet.pdf"
  },
  "registrationDate": "2026-02-10T14:18:00Z",
  "parseResultIds": ["parse-001", "parse-002"]
}
```

**Asset hierarchy (Phase 10.2).** `parentAssetId` (nullable) enables single-party Asset trees. Constraints:

- A child Asset MUST share its parent's owner — cross-party hierarchy is forbidden. Validation runs at registration time (`makeAssetRegistrationArtifacts`).
- The parent must already exist; cycles are forbidden (`A → B → A` rejected).
- The ownership Disclosure Agreement is unchanged — the Actor still owns the child via the standard internal Full DA. Only the *rendered edge* anchors at the parent Asset instead of the Actor, so the canvas reads as a tree (parent on the left, children to the right).
- Claims referencing a parent Asset do NOT implicitly include child Assets. Each Claim's `referencedAssetIds` lists exactly the Assets it references.
- Counterparties NEVER see parent/child relationships — hierarchy is owner-only (see §6).

### 10.2 Parse Result artifact

```json
{
  "artifactType": "parseResult",
  "artifactUri": "provenance://artifacts/parse-{id}",
  "owner": "MicroCo",
  "sourceAssetId": "asset-prm",
  "templateId": "tmpl-electronics-component",
  "templateName": "Electronics Component Profile",
  "templateVersion": 2,
  "fields": [
    { "id": "f-voltage", "name": "Operating voltage", "value": "3.3V ±5%", "confidence": 0.95 }
  ],
  "parseDate": "2026-02-20T15:47:00Z"
}
```

### 10.3 Claim artifact

> **Prototype note — Claim field authority.** Platform-issued: `pin`, `createdDate`, each `amendments[].date`. App-derived / actor-input: `name`, `description`, `referencedAssetIds[]`, `acknowledgments[]`, `referencedRequirementsSets[]` (§10.3a), `amendments[].added[]`, `amendments[].removed[]`, `amendments[].addedRequirementsSetIds[]`, `amendments[].removedRequirementsSetIds[]`. **Authority:** DPP (Platform-issued fields and amendment chain registration).

`acknowledgments` is an array of pre-set terms the Claim owner authors when creating (or amending) the Claim. Each entry is `{ id, title, description }`. Surface to requesters as required-checkbox gates: any DA, EA, or combined request flow against this Claim must show the entire `acknowledgments[]` set as checkboxes; the requester must check every box before Submit enables. The ids of the boxes the requester checked are recorded on the resulting EA's `acknowledgmentsAccepted` field (§10.5) as audit trail. Acknowledgments are owner-authored — they're how the Claim owner pre-encodes terms requesters must acknowledge before the agreement-grant ceremony begins.

```json
{
  "artifactType": "claim",
  "artifactUri": "provenance://claims/{id}",
  "pin": "PIN-0x...",
  "owner": "MicroCo",
  "name": "Power Regulation Module Assembly",
  "description": "Certified assembly backed by datasheet, test report, and thermal analysis.",
  "referencedAssetIds": ["asset-prm-datasheet", "asset-prm-testreport"],
  "acknowledgments": [
    {
      "id": "ack-claim-prm-assembly-1",
      "title": "Result confidentiality",
      "description": "Evaluation results are for internal use only and will not be shared with third parties."
    }
  ],
  "referencedRequirementsSets": [
    { "requirementsSetId": "reqset-mil-prf-55681-v1", "addedDate": "2026-03-01T10:01:00Z" }
  ],
  "createdDate": "2026-03-01T10:00:00Z",
  "amendments": [
    {
      "date": "2026-04-05T12:00:00Z",
      "added": ["asset-prm-thermal"],
      "removed": [],
      "addedRequirementsSetIds": [],
      "removedRequirementsSetIds": []
    }
  ]
}
```

### 10.3a Referenced Standards (Phase 12.1 #120)

`referencedRequirementsSets` is a non-binding metadata array declaring which Requirements Sets the Claim is **built to satisfy**. Strictly informational — does not couple to evaluation, does not auto-suggest in Run Evaluation, and does not produce notifications when changed.

**Entry shape:** `{ requirementsSetId: string, addedDate: ISO8601 }`. The `requirementsSetId` is the version-pinned RS id (e.g. `reqset-mil-prf-55681-v1`), not a lineage id. References stay pointed at the originally-referenced version even after the Library author supersedes the RS — drift is surfaced via the "Newer version available" pill on the Detail Panel row, not via auto-retargeting.

**Authority:** owner-authoritative. Only the Claim owner can add or remove references. A Claim does NOT inherit references from its referenced Assets. The pool of selectable RS for the picker is the union of (owner's authored RS in their Library) + (publicly published RS in the Public Directory). Privately-shared RS from third parties are out of scope.

**Edit pathways:**
- **Create-time:** picker section in `V22CreateClaimModal`. Optional, defaults to `[]`.
- **Add / remove (canonical):** `AmendClaimModal` — adds an amendment record with `addedRequirementsSetIds[]` / `removedRequirementsSetIds[]` populated. Cascade-skip: does NOT mark prior Eval Results stale and does NOT generate notifications. Amendment record is logged for audit only.
- **Inline supersession update (scoped exception):** `UpdateRSReferenceModal` opens from the "Newer version available" pill on a single row (owner only). Confirm records an amendment with diff = removed `[oldVersionId]`, added `[latestVersionId]` and updates the array entry's `requirementsSetId` to the latest version with a fresh `addedDate`. Update jumps to the latest in the supersession chain (not the next link). Each row updated independently — no batch action. This is the **only** inline mutation affordance on the Referenced Standards section; the precedent does not extend to other Detail Panel sections.

**Cross-role behavior:** the field travels with the Claim artifact, so every viewer (owner + grantees of any DA on the Claim) sees the same `referencedRequirementsSets` data. Non-owners see the "Newer version available" pill as informational text only — not clickable.

**Detail Panel rendering:** `V22ClaimPanel` renders a "Referenced Standards" section after Referenced Assets and before Acknowledgments. Each row shows the RS name (clickable → opens Library at the originally-referenced version), a provenance badge ("Authored by you" / "Public"), an optional "Newer version available" pill when the referenced version is not the latest in its lineage, and the `addedDate` in muted small text. Empty state omits the section entirely.

### 10.3b Asset versioning chain on a Claim (Phase 12.2 #122)

Phase 12.2 introduced Claim-internal Asset versioning to support evidence amendment without compromising prior evaluations' audit trail. The model is parallel to §10.3a's RS supersession (version-pinned + chain-walked) but scoped to a Claim's reference list rather than a Library artifact.

**Field shape:** `assetReferences: [{ assetId, supersededBy, addedDate, removedDate }]`. The active `referencedAssetIds[]` (primitive string array) stays the read-side surface for the 42+ existing consumers — `assetReferences[]` is the audit chain that AmendClaim, OUTDATED detection, and re-run diff helpers walk. `referencedAssetIds[]` is derived from `assetReferences[]` as the entries with `removedDate === null && supersededBy === null` (the active heads).

**AmendClaim affordances** (owner only):
- **Replace:** opens a successor picker (Asset pool: owner's own Assets not already on the Claim). Selection stamps `supersededBy` on the existing entry and appends a fresh entry for the replacement (`supersededBy: null, addedDate: now, removedDate: null`). The Asset node itself is NEVER modified.
- **Remove:** stamps `removedDate: now` on the existing entry without successor.
- Both produce identical OUTDATED signals on prior Eval Results referencing the affected Asset.

**OUTDATED Eval Result lifecycle:**
- New `status` value `'outdated'` alongside existing `'active'` and `'superseded'`.
- `isEvalResultStale(evalResult, claim)` walks `evidenceUsed` against the post-amendment chain; returns true if any used Asset has been superseded or removed.
- AmendClaim submit walks all Eval Results on the Claim, calls the helper, flips newly-stale ones to `'outdated'`, and enqueues a `v22-eval-result-stale` notification (single-grantee, informational) on the evaluator's inbox.
- Notification click pans to the Eval Result and dismisses the row; OUTDATED status persists until re-run.
- Re-run produces a new active Eval Result (via #117's flow); the prior OUTDATED result transitions to `'superseded'` — supersession outranks outdated.

**Visual treatment:**
- AssetNode renders an amber `OUTDATED` badge in Row 0 (same row as REVOKED) + dashed amber card border. Distinguished from PROVISIONAL (dashed grey) and REVOKED (solid red).
- Eval Result Detail Panel renders an "Out of date" notice section near the top with re-run guidance. Cross-referenced from the standard Re-Run Evaluation footer button.

### 10.4 Disclosure Agreement artifact

> **Prototype note — Disclosure Agreement field authority.** Platform-issued: agreement `id`, `terms.createdDate`, `terms.expires` (once set; validity enforced Platform-side), `status`, each `amendments[]` entry's metadata. App-derived / actor-input: `grantor`, `grantee`, `subject`, `granteeAssetId`, `type`, `scope`, `terms.autoRenew`. In production, the agreement itself is provisioned by SDP, signed by both parties, and stored bilaterally with Platform-verified cryptographic consent records. The `status` field transitions (`active` → `expired`, `active` → `revoked`, `pending` → `active`, etc.) are managed by SDP as lifecycle state changes. **Authority:** SDP (provisioning, lifecycle state, status transitions, bilateral consent recording); DPP (agreement artifact registration).

The Disclosure Agreement's `subject` field identifies the artifact being disclosed. It replaces V2.1's `claimId` field, which was ambiguous for ownership (Actor → Asset), claim-internal (Claim → referenced Asset), proof-of-evaluation (Eval Result → Claim), and public-directory variants. `subject.kind` is one of `'asset' | 'claim' | 'evalResult' | 'parseResult'`.

```json
{
  "artifactType": "disclosureAgreement",
  "artifactUri": "provenance://agreements/disclosure-{id}",
  "grantor": { "party": "MicroCo", "dot": "DOT-0x..." },
  "grantee": { "party": "GovCo", "dot": "DOT-0x..." },
  "subject": { "kind": "claim", "id": "claim-prm-assembly" },
  "granteeAssetId": "asset-bob-avionics",
  "type": "full",
  "scope": {
    "assetIds": ["asset-prm-datasheet", "asset-prm-testreport"],
    "fieldIds": null,
    "evaluationResultIds": null,
    "includeDerivatives": true
  },
  "terms": {
    "createdDate": "2026-03-04T16:42:00Z",
    "expires": "2027-03-04T16:42:00Z",
    "autoRenew": false
  },
  "amendments": [],
  "status": "active"
}
```

Typical subject assignments across DA variants:

| Variant | subject | Edge endpoints (Phase 2 derivation) |
|---|---|---|
| Actor → Asset ownership | `{ kind: 'asset', id }` | grantor's Actor node ↔ subject |
| Actor → Claim ownership | `{ kind: 'claim', id }` | grantor's Actor node ↔ subject |
| Claim → referenced Asset (internal) | `{ kind: 'claim', id }` + `scope.assetIds: [assetId]` | subject ↔ each `scope.assetIds` entry |
| Inter-party Claim disclosure | `{ kind: 'claim', id }` + `granteeAssetId` | subject ↔ `granteeAssetId` |
| Public-directory disclosure | `{ kind: 'claim', id }` + grantee = Radiant Network | subject ↔ Radiant Network node |
| Proof-of-Evaluation | `{ kind: 'evalResult', id }` | subject ↔ the Claim it evaluated (resolved via the Eval Result's `claimId`) |
| Eval Result → evaluator's Asset (ownership) | `{ kind: 'evalResult', id }` + `scope.assetIds: [assetId]` | subject ↔ each `scope.assetIds` entry |
| Parse Result → source Asset (internal) | `{ kind: 'parseResult', id }` + `scope.assetIds: [sourceAssetId]` | subject ↔ each `scope.assetIds` entry (Phase 4 pre-fix; one seeded per Parse Result so `parse → asset` edges render on the owner's canvas) |

#### Grantee view derivation per disclosure type

When a non-owner viewer (the grantee) opens a disclosed Claim's Detail Panel, the panel's "Referenced Assets" section is derived from the active inter-party DA where the viewer is grantee, branched by `type`:

- **`type='full'`** — the section lists every Asset id in `scope.assetIds`. Asset rows show name + Expand button only (no field count). Expand opens the Asset Evidence Viewer (file metadata header + iframe-based PDF when `file.localPath` is set).
- **`type='selective'`** — the section lists every Asset id in `scope.assetIds`, with each row annotated by the count of disclosed parsed fields. The count is derived by intersecting the DA's `scope.fieldIds` (each entry is `${parseResultId}::${fieldId}`) with the Parse Results derived from the Asset (`pr.sourceAssetId === asset.id`). The section header's count reflects the disclosed Asset count (`referencedAssetNames.length`), NOT the Claim's full `referencedAssetIds.length` — a Selective grantee whose DA covers 1 of 3 referenced Assets sees "Referenced Assets (1)". Expand opens a parsed-fields table — the underlying file is NOT disclosed under Selective, so the file viewer is suppressed in favor of the row-level disclosure. The Expand modal's JSON tab also branches: instead of the full Asset artifact (which carries `file.{filename, hash, mimeType, size, uri, localPath}`), Selective viewers see a disclosed-portion-only view (`{ assetId, name, owner, disclosureType, disclosedFields }`).
- **`type='proofonly'`** (Phase 11D.3, layout refined in 11D.4.1) — Assets are NOT exposed at all. The Claim's Detail Panel renders the Referenced Assets section with a `(0)` count and a proof-only-specific empty hint ("No Assets disclosed under this agreement."). The DA's payload is `scope.evaluationResultIds`: each chosen Eval Result is pulled onto the grantee's canvas as a parent-layer node and connected to the source Claim via a proof-only-styled edge. The Claim itself is also pulled in (no paired EA required — proof-only is a standalone disclosure type). The conventional Claim ↔ `granteeAssetId` anchor edge still renders so the pulled Claim has a visual home on the grantee's canvas. Pulled-in Eval Results are positioned RELATIVE to their source Claim (Phase 11D.4.1) — `(sourceClaim.x + 200, sourceClaim.y + 300)` — rather than at a fixed column. The Eval Result hangs off-and-below the Claim so the connecting edge stays short and doesn't cross horizontal traffic lanes used by the actor's own ChipCo content. Multiple ERs disclosed under one DA stack vertically by `COL_Y_OFFSET = 100`. Expand on a pulled-in Eval Result opens the standard `'eval-output'` schema (no disclosed-portion gating — proof-only's whole purpose is to surface the WHAT and the result rows). Defensive: if an Asset row is somehow Expanded under a proof-only DA, the Output tab renders an empty-state message and the JSON tab surfaces only `{ assetId, name, owner, disclosureType: 'proofonly' }` to avoid leaking file metadata.

The owner viewing their own Claim sees all referenced Assets unconditionally with no field count (full access); Expand opens the standard file viewer.

**§6 cross-canvas effect:** proof-only Claim DAs pull both the Claim AND the chosen Eval Results onto the grantee's canvas. No Asset nodes are pulled in (Assets aren't exposed under proof-only). The grantee can navigate the pulled-in Eval Result's Detail Panel — Owner section shows the evaluator's party and the linked Claim name; Results section lists each requirement row with status badges.

### 10.5 Evaluation Agreement artifact

> **Prototype note — Evaluation Agreement field authority.** Platform-issued: agreement `id`, `terms.createdDate`, `status`, `disclosureAgreementId` linkage. App-derived / actor-input: `grantor`, `grantee`, `claimId`, `granteeAssetId`, `authorizedRequirementsSetIds`, `restrictions`, `terms.evaluationDeadline`, `terms.resultExpiry`, `terms.flowDownRequirements`, `incentives`. **Authority:** SDP (provisioning, lifecycle, status transitions) — the Evaluation Agreement is SDP-managed in the same way as the paired Disclosure Agreement.

The `authorizedRequirementsSetIds` field is **advisory / informational**, not enforced. It records the Requirements Sets the requester suggested when sending the original combined request, so the grantor sees the requester's stated intent during the response flow. Once an Evaluation Agreement exists, the grantee may run **any** Requirements Set from their library against the Claim — the platform does not gate evaluation by this list. (Earlier Round 11 spec drafts implied enforcement; the product decision in Phase 6 was that gating evaluation by an authorized list adds friction without meaningfully improving trust.)

`terms.evaluationDeadline` is the EA's expiry date. The responder sets this when accepting (CombinedResponseModal step 3 — defaults to one year from response date). The prototype also enforces a demo-only expiry check at evaluation time — `Run Evaluation` refuses to open if the deadline is in the past, with a copy hint pointing the user toward requesting a new agreement. Production will replace this with a platform-level policy check. Passive expiry notifications (`v22-ea-expiring-soon`, `v22-ea-expired`) are deferred (#133).

> **Prototype note — Architectural correction (Phase 11C.1).** Earlier Phase 11C drafts placed `resultConfidentiality` and `attribution` as requester-authored booleans on `terms`. That conflated the agreement's terms (responder-authored — the Claim owner sets the conditions of the agreement) with acknowledgments (requester-authored — they accept pre-set commitments before the request goes out). The 11C.1 cleanup moves the acknowledgments off `terms` entirely and replaces them with a top-level `acknowledgmentsAccepted` field that holds the ids of the Claim's pre-set `acknowledgments[]` (§10.3) the requester checked. The agreement's actual terms remain responder-authored.

`acknowledgmentsAccepted` is a top-level array of acknowledgment ids — references to the Claim's `acknowledgments[]` array (see §10.3). Audit trail of what the requester acknowledged when sending the request. Carries through unchanged from provisional EA → active EA on accept; the responder can review but cannot mutate the requester's acknowledgments.

```json
{
  "artifactType": "evaluationAgreement",
  "artifactUri": "provenance://agreements/evaluation-{id}",
  "grantor": { "party": "MicroCo", "dot": "DOT-0x..." },
  "grantee": { "party": "GovCo", "dot": "DOT-0x..." },
  "claimId": "claim-prm-assembly",
  "granteeAssetId": "asset-bob-avionics",
  "disclosureAgreementId": "disclosure-001",
  "authorizedRequirementsSetIds": ["req-mil-prf-55681-v1"],
  "acknowledgmentsAccepted": ["ack-claim-prm-assembly-1", "ack-claim-prm-assembly-2"],
  "restrictions": {
    "priorEvaluationRequired": null,
    "additionalParticipants": []
  },
  "terms": {
    "createdDate": "2026-03-04T16:42:00Z",
    "evaluationDeadline": "2026-04-04T16:42:00Z",
    "resultExpiry": null,
    "flowDownRequirements": []
  },
  "incentives": {
    "onSatisfactory": "Certificate of compliance issued to grantee",
    "onUnsatisfactory": null
  },
  "status": "active"
}
```

### 10.6 Evaluation Result artifact

> **Prototype note — Evaluation Result field authority.** Platform-issued: `pin`, `ownerDot`, `evaluationDate`, `status`, `supersededBy` links, the Proof-of-Evaluation (POE) attestation that this artifact represents. App-derived / actor-input: `evaluationAgreementId`, `claimId`, `granteeAssetId`, `requirementsSet` (embedded snapshot), `results[]` (per-row values and assessments), `evidenceUsed[]`. In production, the Evaluation Result is produced by REP executing the Requirements Set against the in-scope evidence; the result artifact is the POE itself, cryptographically signed and on-chain, providing verifiable credibility scoring for downstream trust decisions. **Authority:** REP (execution, result production, POE issuance); DPP (artifact registration); SDP (supersede chain lifecycle).

```json
{
  "artifactType": "evaluationResult",
  "artifactUri": "provenance://artifacts/eval-{id}",
  "pin": "PIN-0x...",
  "owner": "GovCo",
  "ownerDot": "DOT-0x...",
  "evaluationAgreementId": "eval-agreement-001",
  "claimId": "claim-prm-assembly",
  "granteeAssetId": "asset-bob-avionics",
  "requirementsSet": {
    "id": "req-mil-prf-55681-v1",
    "name": "MIL-PRF-55681 Compliance",
    "version": 1
  },
  "results": [
    { "requirementId": "req-001", "label": "Power output stability", "value": "3.3V ±0.5%", "status": "satisfactory" },
    { "requirementId": "req-004", "label": "Radiation tolerance", "value": "TID > 100 krad(Si)", "status": "unsatisfactory" }
  ],
  "evidenceUsed": ["asset-prm-datasheet", "asset-prm-testreport"],
  "evaluationDate": "2026-03-09T14:32:00Z",
  "status": "active",
  "supersededBy": null
}
```

---

## 11. State Transitions

### 11.1 Claim amendment

Alice amends her Claim by adding one or more Asset references. Preconditions: she owns the Claim; she owns the Assets being added.

1. Alice selects a Claim node; footer action "Amend Claim" opens a modal.
2. She selects additional Assets from her canvas (or creates new ones inline).
3. Modal confirms the amendment.
4. Claim's JSON is updated with a new `amendments[]` entry (added Asset IDs, timestamp).
5. No Disclosure Agreements are automatically updated. Alice must explicitly amend each agreement to share the new evidence.

> **Prototype note — Claim amendment.** The prototype writes the `amendments[]` entry directly to the Claim artifact in `v22Provisionals` on submit. Counterparties don't see Claim amendments directly — they only learn of new content when the grantor amends the **Disclosure Agreement** to include the new Assets/fields (see §11.2 and the `v22-da-amendment` notification in §7.4). DA amendment is the user-visible event; Claim amendment is internal Claim-owner state. (Phase 11E.2 briefly shipped a `v22-claim-amendment` fan-out, rolled back in Phase 11E.4 after the conceptual review.) **In production:** Claim amendments are DPP lifecycle events on the existing Claim artifact; each amendment is cryptographically recorded and the amendment chain is verifiable on-chain. **Authority:** DPP (amendment registration + event emission).

### 11.2 Disclosure Agreement amendment

Alice amends a Disclosure Agreement to include (or exclude) evidence.

1. Alice clicks the Agreement Edge between her Claim and a counterparty's Asset. Selects "Disclosure Agreement" from the edge menu.
2. Disclosure Agreement Detail Panel opens. Action: "Amend Disclosure."
3. Modal shows current scope; she adjusts which Assets/fields/eval results are included.
4. **Evidence can only be added in an amendment, not removed** — if the Disclosure has been used for an evaluation. (Evidence can be removed if no evaluation has been run against it yet. Final rule TBD; default is no removal.)
5. Counterparty receives a notification that the agreement was amended. Updated scope is immediately available.

> **Prototype note — Disclosure Agreement amendment.** The prototype writes the amended scope directly to the agreement artifact in `v22Provisionals` and fires a `v22-da-amendment` notification to the grantee. **Phase 11E.2 ship (#102):** the notification type was renamed from `v22-amendment` → `v22-da-amendment` (parallel to `v22-ea-amendment` and `v22-claim-amendment`); the click handler now pans to the Claim and opens the DA Detail Panel directly via `setOpenAgreement({ kind: 'disclosure', disclosureAgreementId })`, mirroring the EA amendment routing.
